# Scrub audit — everything agents built since 2026-05 (2026-10-14)

**Scope.** Every repository in the composite (kzen-lib, kzen-auto, kzen-project, kzen-launcher, kzen-shell, kzen-sample,
kzen-repo) and the written guidance (AGENTS.md files, architecture docs, coding standards, plans, analyses, memory).
Agents have added about 237k lines since 2026-05-01: kzen-auto +179k, kzen-lib +36k, kzen-sample +16k, the
launcher, shell and project +7.6k. Code from before 2026-05-01 is the user's own work and is judged only where agents
changed it.

**Status.** Read-only. No code or doc has been changed because of this audit, and no build or test was run for it.
Nothing below is acted on until the user decides.

## How to read this

The audit is organised by **what is wrong**, not by who asked for what:
- the behaviour is broken;
- the same thing is implemented more than once;
- limits and timing guesses were invented;
- there are hidden modes, leftovers, and test code in production;
- generality was built that nothing uses;
- docs are false or steer agents wrong.

Those defects stand on their own. The same broken rule implemented independently in five places is a problem whatever
anyone asked for. Each row in the appendices still notes its provenance, but only as context. It is used for one thing:
**protecting choices the user made** (§7), so that a cleanup does not undo them.

Verdicts in the appendix tables: **Keep**, **Simplify** (says how), **Delete**, **User decision** (states the question).

**How the claims were checked.**
- Ten area auditors read the code and cite `file:line` for every claim; claims they could not confirm are marked
  "unverified" or "inferred" in their rows.
- Before this file was written, the most serious claims were re-checked by hand. Marked ✔ below, all confirmed:
  - the run failing past the 256-file sample;
  - snapshot cycle detection rejecting shared values;
  - the trace timestamp being query time;
  - rename rewriting plain text;
  - spring-embed's 10-argument `KzenAutoConfig` call against 11 parameters;
  - the shell's `context.start()` outside its `try`;
  - the YAML save swallowing errors;
  - the unauthenticated `/kzen-host/shutdown`.
- One auditor claim was wrong and is corrected: the Format table's `meta` row is the user's choice (§7).

**Evidence used for provenance.**
- The user's 1,618 typed kzen prompts (2026-05 → 2026-10-14).
- Full session logs from 2026-09-15 on, including 28 answers to agents' questions and plan approvals.
- Earlier answers and approvals were not recorded, and other agents worked on the code too. A "Not traced" note
  therefore means "no record found", nothing more.

---

## 1. Broken today

These are wrong behaviours, not style issues. The code under each is cited in its area appendix (letter in brackets).

1. ✔ **Runs fail when a column first appears after the 256th file, and validating again cannot fix it**
   [B, C].
   - Before Run, File offers only `files.take(256)` (`FileSourceWorker.kt:122-123`).
   - Parse types the output from that sample.
   - JobRun passes the type to the run as the validated contract (`JobRun.kt:312`).
   - `DataReadCore.fitShape` (`:348-384`) then fails any later file with a field outside it: "validate the Job
     again". Revalidating reads the same 256.
   - That a run keeps its validated type was approved (R5, 2026-09-24). On 2026-10-07 the user asked for the
     Report's behaviour instead: pre-check every file's header before the run. Which one holds is question 3 in §8.
     Under either, the 256 sample cap must not decide the run's type.
2. ✔ **Values that share a container fail as a "cycle"** [H].
   - `DataSnapshot.seenIdentities` is never popped (`DataSnapshot.kt:126,178-181`).
   - So a value reached twice is rejected, even when it is shared and not cyclic.
   - Through `EngineJobControl.kt:312-320` this fails the Job. The check is also O(n²).
3. ✔ **Large Job results that hold an open resource fail on invented snapshot caps** [H, C].
   - A result that holds an object the run will close (an open stream, say) is deep-copied into plain data before
     the run ends (`EngineJobControl.snapshot`, `:304-320`, called from `ResultSinkWorker.kt:110`). Results without
     such objects are passed through untouched.
   - The copy fails the Job past `SnapshotPolicy`'s defaults: depth 64, 100k elements, 1 MB text, 10 MB binary
     and 5 s.
4. ✔ **Renaming a step or Job parameter rewrites ordinary text** [F].
   - `KzenAutoCodeReferenceRewriter.kt:66-74` takes every scalar value attribute whose text lexes to the old name,
     e.g. a BrowserWriteStep's typed `text`.
   - In a Job, renaming a parameter does this document-wide.
5. ✔ **kzen-sample spring-embed does not compile against current kzen-auto** [G].
   - `KzenAutoConfig` has 11 parameters; `codeCacheRoot` was added 2026-09-25 for tests only.
   - `KzenWorkspace.java:88-98` passes 10, so `services` lands in the `Path?` slot.
6. ✔ **Trace timestamps are the time of the query, not of the event** [H].
   - `RunEngineLogicTrace.kt:129,157,189` stamps `Clock.System.now()` when the trace is queried.
   - `objectStableId` is the document's id, not the step's.
7. **"Ask again until done" can loop forever** [C].
   - A pass gives up once its 2.5 s deadline has passed, and File's folder listing / Read's manifest are re-done
     uncached in every pass.
   - So a listing that alone takes ≥ 2.5 s never progresses. The editor re-asks every 1 s forever
     (`JobController.kt:499-507`).
   - Today's uncommitted test harness has the same `while (true)`.
8. **The compiled-code cache can replay stale jars or errors** [G].
   - The key is class name + source digest only, while compiles include plugin jars.
   - Entries persist across restarts, Kotlin/kzen upgrades and test contexts.
   - Its comment says it "can never be stale".
9. ✔ **The desktop shell hangs on "Loading..." if the launcher fails to start** [I].
   - `KzenShellMain.kt:28` calls `context.start()` outside the `try`.
   - The readiness poll uses `HttpURLConnection` with no timeouts, so a silent child is never killed.
10. ✔ **Saving notation silently drops to a full rewrite on any error** [H]. `YamlNotationParser.kt:115-120` catches
    every exception from the format-preserving save, with no log.
11. **`.txt` / `.log` / `.md` members of an archive fail; the same files on disk read fine** [A]. The cause is
    `FilenameDetection.formatFor` (`:101`) → `ParseWorker.kt:477`.
12. **BOM "detect" behaves exactly like "require"** but is labelled "Detect charset" [A].
13. **The Channel / Duplex Channel ribbon tools insert objects the page never shows**, so they cannot be seen or
    removed. `SelectChannelEditor` is bound but never mounted [D].
14. **The Filter and Pivot value pickers are empty after a run ends or the page reloads** [D]. They read only the
    live in-memory store (inferred from code).
15. **`TarGzEntryCursor` keeps every archive entry it ever produced** (`produced` list, test-only), so memory grows
    with the member count [B].
16. **Output depends on machine load**:
    - Preview's 50 ms per-item capture budget [B];
    - Flow's 100 ms snapshot budget and its 50 ms "user is stepping" guess [F];
    - Report's 200 ms trace throttle, which has no final flush [E].
17. **Slow motion treats a step as settled after 30 s** and may send the next Step while one is still running. One
    late first SSE message turns push off until the page reloads [G].
18. ✔ **spring-embed ships unauthenticated `POST /kzen-host/shutdown` and `/kzen-host/budget/hold`** [I]. Any page
    open in the browser can trigger them.
19. **ForEach returns `[]` whenever static analysis finds nothing reading the loop** [F].
20. **Hosted Report is broken for preview and run directories**, as its own KDoc admits [E].
21. **`ColumnValue` equals/hashCode disagree** (epsilon vs text) [E]. This predates the agents, but it is now only
    commented on, not fixed.
22. **The itch core decides gzip by magic bytes even after the host has inflated `.gz` by name** [I].

| Reading gzip | <ul><li>Commons Compress wrapper (Job)</li><li>JDK `GZIPInputStream` (Report)</li><li>A separate tar.gz cursor</li><li>Extract assumes every input is tar.gz</li></ul> | A, B, E |
| Writing files | <ul><li>CSV Writer, Export, and Format+Write: three paths</li><li>MiGz gzip writing coded twice</li><li>CSV encoding coded twice: commons-csv, plus a hand-written encoder that "follows it byte for byte"</li></ul> | B, E |

| Job vs Report | Job imports about 15 Report classes and 10 Report specs. Specs were bent to carry Job-only concepts. The "DS0" move only renamed packages | E |

## 3. Invented limits and timing guesses

None of these numbers traces to the user. The user said on 2026-10-09: "there's no cap -- please don't make up stuff
that I didn't ask for".

One exception in principle: the 2026-09-24 problem definition the user approved says looking at data before Run "is
bounded in time and size, reused when unchanged, refreshed when the data changes, and never holds up editing" (R4).
So a design-time bound of some kind was approved. Its numbers were not: the same text left "Limits" as an open
question, and it was never answered (question 2 in §8). Two of the bounds the user asked for are in §7.

| Limit | Where | Effect | Step 2 (2026-10-15) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 256 values / 2.5 s editor / 30 s run | `DesignReadBudget.kt:20-23` | Partial types, the endless re-ask, and the run failure in §1.1. A bound before Run is approved (R4); these numbers, and using one at run time, are not | Removed |
| 256 files, 4 concurrent, 64 MiB, 15 s per source | `SourceFormatResolutionPolicy`, `SourceFormatResolutionBudget` | Wraps explicit formats and listings too; its error says to "raise" a policy nothing can set | Removed in step 1 |
| 256 KiB sample, 100 records, 2 s detection | `DetectionPolicy.kt:33-43` | Re-run per file at run time | Removed in step 1 |
| 1 MiB per record, 1 MiB per field, 10,000 fields | `ReaderExecutionPolicies.kt:15-21` | Fails real files on every full run | Removed |
| Snapshot depth 64, 100k elements, 1 MB / 10 MB, 5 s | kzen-lib `SnapshotPolicy.kt:13-19` | Fails large Job results (§1.3) | Removed, with `ReadBudget` / `ReadPreparation` |
| Deadlock monitor 50 ms poll, fail after ~200 ms, stall log ~2 s | `JobDeadlockMonitor` | Decides "deadlock" by counting, not by a wait-for cycle | Removed: a deadlocked run waits until cancelled |
| External query 1 s ("KNOWN BOUNDED SEAM") | `JobRun.kt:377-394` | UI queries to a slow Worker return nothing | Removed: waits off the controller monitor; fails when the channel closes |
| Progress throttle 200 ms (Job), trace throttle 200 ms (Report) | B, E | Stale counters; no final flush in Report | Kept, for a decision; Job progress is now transient. Report not touched (area E) |
| Preview: limit 200, 50 chars per cell, 50 ms per item, 256 KiB, 2,000 nodes, 8 MiB | `PreviewSampleView`, `PreviewCapture` | Truncated, nondeterministic previews | Removed; kept for a decision: the 50-char expandable cell and the 64-byte binary excerpt |
| Validation re-fetch every 1 s while partial; stale retry 100 ms × 10 | `JobController`, `ServerValidationFetch` | Endless polling (§1.7) | Re-fetch removed; the stale retry went in step 1 |
| Summary values 5 / 5 / 40 | Summary card | — | Removed: one line, cut by width, full text as tooltip |
| `migzThreads = 7` | `WriteWorker`, `ExportCompression` | Fixed thread count | Removed in Write; `ExportCompression` not touched (area E) |
| Compiled-code disk cache 1 GiB + LRU walk after every compile; validation cache 100; instance cache 32 | G, F | Unrequested eviction | Disk budget and LRU removed; in-memory caches kept, for a decision |
| ForEach journal 100 × 120 chars; trace display 2,048 chars; Flow/Script snapshot depth 16, 256 elements, 100 ms | F | Hidden truncation | Removed. The 100-entry journal went in FJ (2026-10-15): every entry is kept in a persistent list beside the trace and read by range, at a constant cost per iteration and per refresh |
| SSE 3 s probe, 45 s watchdog, 10 s poll; 10 s close cutoff; slow-motion 30 s | G | §1.17 | Close cutoff and slow-motion cap removed; SSE timings kept, for a decision |
| Shell 120 s child kill, 60 s / 10 s proxy, 10 MB log; launcher 128-character names | I | The plan said 90 s and overridable; the code is fixed | Not touched (area I) |
| spring-embed 5 s / 30 s / 6 h timeouts, 4 GiB budget (the plan said 256 MiB) | I | — | Not touched (area I) |
| itch core 512 MiB build budget, 8 MiB / 30 s prefetch, 1 MiB fingerprint hashing | I | — | Not touched (area I) |

## 4. Hidden modes, leftovers, and test code in production

- **Unused or hidden settings:**
  - `contentCodings`, which the format materializer authors and which then overrides the name;
  - `catalogVisible`;
  - the dead charset `auto` path;
  - Target match policies `first`/`nth`/`best` (no UI; only a test YAML);
  - the `instanceCaching` opt-out and a second unbounded `GraphInstanceCache` mode;
  - the `context.slots` compatibility warning;
  - the legacy `CsvReaderWorker`/`MultiFileReaderWorker`, despite "we don't need backwards compatibility"
    (2026-09-24);
  - `RunLogicVertex2`, a duplicate archetype kept to avoid a grid-geometry change.
- **Spike toggles read on hot paths:** `EngineJobControl.drainingEnabled` (spike CS3) and
  `JobChannel.consumerCloseEnabled` (CS2), plus "spike CS2" labels in the data openers.
- **Test seams in main:**
  - `TarGzEntryCursor.observer`/`produced`;
  - `WriteWorker.encoderInterceptor`;
  - `PreviewWorker.captureNanos`;
  - `FlowRun.snapshotMillis`;
  - `KzenAutoContext.forTest`, `TestContextLeakGuard`, `codeCacheRoot` and test-only `WorkUtils` factories;
  - the launcher's `ShellSimulator` "fail"/"exit" names;
  - spring-embed's shutdown and hold endpoints;
  - itch benchmarks and `ItchEncoder` in the main jar.
- **Fake gates and kits:**
  - `PluginSpiVersion` is still 1 after several SPI changes, and a manifest without `spi` is accepted;
  - `PluginCompatibilityKit` is a 410-line CLI with 10 `--expect-*` flags.
- **Workarounds kept alive:**
  - Report's no-op trace handle and reserved `$trace-path` address;
  - the LMAX MULTI-producer switch made to quiet a test;
  - `RetryingZipCloser`;
  - the format-preserving YAML fallback (§1.10).
- **An approved workaround:** `RemoteApplyGate` works around a kzen-lib store ordering race on the client. The user
  approved that plan on 2026-09-17, including "No kzen-lib change, no protocol change". It stays unless the user
  wants the race fixed in kzen-lib instead (question 10 in §8).

## 5. Generality nothing uses

- The **content-probe plugin API** (`ReaderProbeCapability`, `ReaderProbeStrength.ContentSignature` "magic
  bytes", `ReaderProbeRequest`, `ReaderProbeObserver`, `StrictCharacterView`), plus the detection-only attributes on
  every format.
  - It is live in production, and both kzen-sample readers implement it.
- **kzen-lib type-system and engine extras with no product consumer:**
  - union/variant types;
  - the native type-resolver SPI;
  - the DataAdapter registry (whose error advises registering an adapter, which is impossible);
  - `NativeTypeDescriber`;
  - sensitive redaction;
  - the `retained` bindings that nothing closes;
  - `retainTrace`/`observeFrames`/`observeResets`;
  - the raw string-keyed resource layer;
  - an empty `closeDataAdapters()`.
- **Others:**
  - `TargetTypeLocator.register` (test-only);
  - the format "correction" layer in the UI (Make explicit / Lock columns / Inspect columns) and its one-implementation
    override-editor SPI;
  - the 493-line hand-written Kotlin lexer under rename (a decision for the user);
  - hand-rolled replacements for libraries: spring-embed's reverse proxy, `WeightedBudget` (not FIFO; a fair
    `Semaphore` would do) and `PersistentSortedMap`.

## 6. Docs that are false or steer agents wrong

- **Content sniffing is still the written design, and nothing marks it superseded:**
  - `docs/analysis/2026-09-02_data-format-detection.md:412-418` ("gzip magic bytes are authoritative");
  - `docs/plans/data-format-detection/README.md` ("Status: complete");
  - `docs/analysis/2026-08-29_data-reading.md:253-256`;
  - `kzen-auto/AGENTS.md:113` ("a binary format is detected by content");
  - `kzen-auto/docs/architecture.md:902-908`.

  The agent review of the detection analysis (`…_review-1.md`, 2026-09-02) endorsed the probe SPI and proposed the
  per-source probe budget behind the 256-file / 15 s limit.
- **Agent-chosen limits are recorded as decisions.** The values-metadata plan says its numbers were "answered as
  defaults" and that "implement this entire plan" "overrode the per-phase design reviews".
- **False facts:**
  - "`useCommonJs()` must stay" (umbrella and kzen-auto AGENTS.md, js-architecture.md): both frontends use ES modules;
  - `architecture.md` documents the deleted Name pattern, `FilterIndex`, `RecordOutputBuilder`,
    `ScriptExecutionContext.referencedValue` and a "filter index" storage area;
  - kzen-lib's `PlatformSynchronized` and `GraphCreator.create()`;
  - the YAML dialect's "self-extinguishing" escapes and its wrong rule for values containing `:`;
  - the plugin module's "zero dependencies" (it has `api` dependencies on common and lib);
  - stale class names in the shell, launcher and project AGENTS.md files;
  - CC-21's BuildInfo example.
- **The plan system contradicts the user's instruction that plans are ephemeral** (2026-07-10: "I don't want the
  plan files to be a source of truth or documentation").
  - There are 84 plan files (~20.9k lines), and 13 passages call a plan "the only record".
  - The ledger is stale: J5b is still ☐; `next/` says it is blocked; five LANDED plans are held "until Sprint 3",
    which doesn't exist.

## 7. Choices the user made, which a cleanup must not undo

Recorded in the user's own words. More are listed in each appendix row marked **Requested**, and in the corrections
appended to this file.
- The Format column table keeps its `meta` row: "actually I changed my mind, I think the `meta` row is fine -- I just
  didn't really understand how it worked" (2026-10-10 18:56).
- File names decide type and compression (2026-10-12, 2026-10-14).
- One way to pick files, no folder scan or Name pattern (2026-10-13).
- gzip reads every member (2026-10-12).
- The Advanced settings of a Worker sit behind a header toggle (2026-10-13).
- A host-enforced memory limit over ITCH symbol-day batches, with closeable streams and items: "that's how the
  global memory limit is enforced at the host" (2026-09-04).
- A bound on the loaded-class cache: "the size shouldn't be unbounded" (2026-07-11 21:57).

Approved by the user on an agent's proposal (the approved text, not every detail around it):
- The Format table's no-contract fallback, "every payload column", kept with the `meta` row (plan approved
  2026-10-10 19:00).
- The 2026-09-24 problem definition:
  - R2: `Read` and `Logic source` keep producing whatever they produce;
  - R4: types inferred from a sample before Run, bounded, cached, refreshed on change, labelled as data-derived
    ("inferred from 41 of 256 files");
  - R5: a run honours the validated type or stops naming the misfit (see §1.1);
  - "No backward compatibility. Saved notation is development and test material only". This is why the legacy
    workers and `paths` in §4 are Delete.
- The Worker base classes as helpers that do not restrict Worker shapes (BE5, 2026-09-17).
- `StepValidation.details` with lenient decoding, the File table's sizes and per-file progress, and the inherited
  channel-knob precedence with its client mirror (2026-10-06).
- `RemoteApplyGate` (2026-09-17, see §4).
- File hands over one file at a time (`batchSize 1`, `capacity 0`) (2026-10-06).
- Unbounded full runs in total bytes read (2026-09-22). This was about total bytes, not per-record or per-field
  caps.

## 8. Questions for the user

These decide what several cleanups do. Each one gates a step in §9. The appendices hold 111 more rows with a
**User decision** verdict (A 6, B 9, C 7, D 4, E 8, F 10, G 17, H 21, I 26, J 3); most are smaller and follow from
the answers below.

1. **Automatic format** [A-1]. Once files are identified by name only, should Automatic stay as a pure
   extension-to-format mapping (`.gz`/`.tgz` removed by name first), or be removed so every Parse names its format,
   with the default picked from the extension?
2. **Looking at data before Run** [A-20, C-6, C-7, D-9]. R4 approves a bound but no numbers. What should it be, if
   anything beyond "never holds up editing"? What should the editor show when it is reached: partial columns with a
   label, or a "Check all files" action? The current behaviour, a re-ask every 1 s that can loop forever, goes
   either way.
3. **Run type** [B-16, C-12]. Should a run pre-check every selected file's header and type itself from that, as the
   Report does (2026-10-07)? Or should it keep the type shown before Run and stop at the first file that doesn't fit
   (R5, 2026-09-24)?
4. **Deadlock monitor** [B-4]. Should a Job fail itself when it counts too many blocked Workers, or only show who is
   waiting on whom, with no thresholds and no automatic failure?
5. **Native ownership** [B-11]. Should a host object be refused when it appears in a second run (the process-wide
   `NativeIdentityRegistry`)? Or is per-run ownership enough, so the global registry is deleted?
6. **CSV reading and writing** [A-35, §2 row 3]. Should the hand-written delimited reader and encoder be replaced by
   commons-csv, which the Report's CSV export already uses? Or kept for speed, with the duplicate encoder removed?
7. **One read path** [A-39, §2 row 7]. Should Report and Job read files through one path? If so, is the base the
   Job's reader or the Report's?
8. **Per-file format copies** [A-34]. Should Override, Make explicit and Lock columns stay? If any one stays, which
   one?
9. **Content provider registry** [A-24]. Should the provider lookup stay for a future S3 source, or collapse to local
   files until S3 is requested?
10. **`RemoteApplyGate`** [G 65]. Keep the approved client-side workaround, or fix the store ordering race in
    kzen-lib?
11. **Snapshot limits at the Job result boundary** [H B-1, §1.3]. Remove `SnapshotPolicy`'s caps entirely, or keep
    depth-only protection against real cycles once cycle detection is fixed (§1.2)?
12. **Rename** [F20, F21, §1.4, §5]. Should rename keep its own Kotlin lexer and rewrite code text, or only rewrite
    attributes that are typed as references?

### Answers (2026-10-14)

- The smaller siblings (area I, and §1.5, §1.9, §1.18, §1.22) are out of scope for now.
- 1: Automatic uses the file extension.
- 2: No artificial limits anywhere. This also settles the numbers questions in §3.
- 3: The full set of columns must be known before a run starts, or header writing breaks. It is cached so files
  are not read again and again.
- 5: One Kzen project per process, or several in one JVM process, must both work.
- 6: Use a library only if it is not slow; otherwise keep a custom parser, or use a different library.
- 7: Report and Job are separate domain concepts. They may share code where the two overlap.
- 9: Arbitrary data sources must be supported: files, HTTP, S3, a random number generator, anything.
- 12: Rename must keep rewriting Kotlin expressions that refer to an `ObjectName`.
- 4: Delete the deadlock auto-fail ("a totally broken feature"). It is off for any Job with a Summary, Pivot,
  Preview or Explore Worker, i.e. most Jobs.
- 5: Delete the global `NativeIdentityRegistry`; keep the per-run ledger that closes closeable items.
- 6: Keep one flexible configured reader (e.g. `;`-delimited files). Its speed against the Report's dedicated
  lexers is unmeasured. The allocation-free `DelimitedRecordEncoder` is accepted; it writes any configured
  delimited format. The other two encoders are `CsvWriterWorker`'s inline quoting and Export's commons-csv path.
- 8: Two ways of reading files (File → Parse, and Read → File data source with per-file format copies) is
  wrong. Keep a general data source concept; the file reading needs re-working.
- CSV Writer Worker: delete (Format → Write covers delimiter, header, columns and result). Export Writer stays
  untouched with Report, which is legacy code.
- 10: The race is real (2026-09-17: "Selected file is unavailable or ambiguous" on a freshly added row), so it gets
  one app-wide fix that replaces both point fixes. `RemoteApplyGate` is a point fix with one consumer (per-file format lookup). Validators fix the same race
  differently (`ValidationDigestEcho` plus a 100 ms × 10 retry). Other requests have neither.
- 11: The Result's automatic copy cannot work for arbitrary object graphs with native handles. Findings:
  - `JobRun.kt:363-368` closes everything the run owns, then returns the result;
  - with `keep: last` every element is copied as it passes;
  - closing is done by two unrelated mechanisms:
    - the engine's scoped disposal, the user's 2026-06/07 design: one registry reached through `bind` with a
      teardown, the raw `resource(key, …)` form, and anonymous `onSettle` (`RunEngine.kt:1831-1843`). Script uses
      it, and so does some Job plumbing;
    - the Job's own `RunOwnershipLedger`, which adopts any `AutoCloseable` that crosses a channel (E9, 2026-09).
      The global registry and the Result copy sit on top of it.
  - Problem definition: `docs/analysis/2026-10-14_resource-policy.md`.
  - User decision (2026-10-14): one standardized, application-wide resource policy, designed deliberately. No
    ad-hoc mechanisms anywhere.
- 12: Rewrite only attributes that hold Kotlin expressions.

## 9. Suggested order of work

1. **File identity by name, everywhere, in one place.** Delete the magic-byte checks, the probe SPI, the content
   probing, `contentCodings` and the UI text, and the itch core's magic check. Add `.tgz`. Supersede the design docs.
   This removes most of §2 row 1 and §1.11, §1.12 and §1.22.
   **Done 2026-10-14, uncommitted** (kzen-auto, kzen-sample, and these docs): the one rule is `FileFormatNames`
   (kzen-auto-common), and the itch core inflates a file only when its name ends in `.gz`.
2. **Remove the invented limits (§3) in one sweep.** That also fixes §1.1, §1.3 and §1.7, and removes the polling.
   **Done 2026-10-15, uncommitted** (kzen-lib, kzen-auto, kzen-sample, and these docs). §3's last column says what
   went and what was kept for a decision.
   - Design time reads every value to the end, with no deadline, and a run uses the full set of columns. The
     "Reading data…" state and the re-ask polling are gone.
   - Reads have no record, field or time limits. A reader's shape is its `fixedShape`, else the cached one, else
     the shape of a cursor it opens. The plugin SPI lost `inspect`, `inspectBlocking`, `policy` and
     `expandedBytesRead`, and `JobControl.reportActivity`.
   - The deadlock monitor, the stall warning and the `$job-ownership` report are gone.
   - A UI request to a Worker waits for the reply; it fails when the run closes the channel.
   - kzen-lib snapshots copy the whole value (`SnapshotPolicy`, `ReadBudget`, `ReadPreparation` deleted; a
     sensitive value is redacted). Script and Flow traces show the whole value.
   - Also removed: the Result, Preview and Summary caps, Write's migz thread count, the compiled-code disk budget
     and LRU, the slow-motion settle cap, the 10 s close cutoff, and the target locator's 8-candidate cap.
3. **Fix the remaining broken items in §1:** snapshot sharing, rename, spring-embed, trace time, compiled-code cache
   key, shell start, YAML save, channel tools, pickers, slow motion, ForEach and Report.
4. **Delete §4 and §5:** test code to test source sets, spike toggles, hidden modes, unused SPIs.
5. **Collapse the duplicates in §2**, starting with Job's dependence on Report and the three file-writing paths.
6. **Rewrite the guidance docs** so they describe the code, and retire plans as a source of truth.

Each step is a separate change for the user to approve. Steps 1 and 2 have been done.

---

# Appendices — area reports

Each appendix is one auditor's full table, with corrections from the provenance re-check applied in place
(marked "corrected"). Row ids are local to their appendix.

## Area A — data reading (kzen-auto)

### Summary
Area A is the kzen-auto file data-reading path: `kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/server/data/**` (content, coding, detection, read, write, format resolution), `server/objects/datasource/**` (FileDataSource, format objects, materialization), `kzen-auto-common/.../common/data/**`, the plugin SPI `kzen-auto-plugin/.../api/data/**` and the notation under `kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/resources/notation/auto-jvm/datasource/`. Roughly 11,200 lines. Almost all of it was written by agents after the baseline (cce82db3f7, 2026-05-01). The only pre-baseline code is the Report input files, which were renamed or moved.

Worst problems:
1. The default format for every file source is **Automatic**, and Automatic decides type and compression by reading the file's bytes. It checks the gzip magic `1f 8b`, rejects ZIP by its `PK` magic, recognises tar by its header magic, and parses the sample as every registered format, the "strongest" match winning. It also infers the encoding and whether a header row exists. This is the design the user rejected on 09-22, 10-12 and 10-14. The 09-22 "by name" request was applied only to static typing, not to the read path.
2. A stack of invented guardrails, none traced to a prompt:
   - per-source resolution budget: 256 files, 4 concurrent, 64 MiB, 15 s, which wraps even explicit-format sources;
   - detection policy: 256 KiB sample, 100 records, 2 s timeout;
   - limits on every full run: 1 MiB per record, 1 MiB per field, 10,000 fields;
   - design-read budget: 256 values / 2.5 s (corrected: a design-time bound itself was in the plan the user approved on 2026-09-24T17:54 UTC, R4 "bounded in time and size"; the numbers were left as an open question and are not traced).

   This contradicts the user on 10-09: "there's no cap -- please don't make up stuff".
3. Gzip is decided in 5 separate places with 3 different rules (by magic, by suffix ignoring case, by suffix with case). The hidden `contentCodings` attribute overrides the suffix rule, and `ContentCodingStack` takes a list but accepts exactly one entry.
4. Hidden or duplicate modes: BOM "detect" behaves exactly like "require" but is labelled "Detect charset". There is a dead `auto` charset path. There is a deprecated `resolvedRead` still on the hot path. Spike labels are left in main.
5. A public plugin probe SPI (`ReaderProbeCapability`, `ReaderProbeStrength.ContentSignature` "magic bytes") builds content identification into the plugin contract.

### Broken or false
1. **Automatic decides gzip by content, not by name.** `DetectionSampleAcquirer.kt:144` (magic `1f 8b`) is the deciding test; the `.gz` suffix only produces a mismatch error (:42). This is the opposite of the user's stated rule and of the claim an agent made that it "went by the name".
2. **The 09-22 by-name request was applied only partly.** `FilenameDetection.declaredShape` (:74) and `formatFor` (:97) go by name, but the on-disk read still probes tar magic (`ArchiveListingReaderCapability`) and gzip magic (row 2).
3. **`.txt` / `.log` / `.md` inside an archive fail, while the same file on disk works.** `FilenameDetection.formatFor` filters `automaticDetectionCandidate` (:101), and PlainTextFormat has `automaticDetectionCandidate=false`. Such members return null, and ParseWorker throws "Parse cannot tell the format of '…' from its name; select a format" (`ParseWorker.kt:477`). On disk, the same name goes to the text fallback.
4. **`.tgz` under Automatic is gzip only by magic.** `hasGzipSuffix` checks only `.gz` (`DetectionSampleAcquirer.kt:141`). Removing magic without adding `.tgz` breaks `.tgz`.
5. **The gzip suffix rules disagree.** `ConfiguredDelimitedFormat.kt:125` and `PlainTextFormat.kt:72` don't strip `?`/`#` (FilenameDetection and the Acquirer do). Report `FileFlatDataStream.kt:17` is case-sensitive, so `X.GZ` is read as plain text in Report.
6. **BOM "detect" is "require".** `CharacterDecoder.kt:62`, `DelimitedProbe.kt:245` and `DelimitedRecordEncoder.kt:139` treat both identically (fail without a BOM; the writer emits one). The UI label "Detect charset" is false.
7. **`ContentCodingStack` takes a list but rejects any list that isn't exactly one entry** ("Exactly one identity or gzip coding is required", `ContentCodingStack.kt:18`).
8. **The error advice is unactionable.** "raise the source-resolution policy" (`SourceFormatResolutionBudget.kt:115`) refers to a policy that no notation or UI can set.
9. **The 15 s / 256-file source deadline wraps explicit formats too.** `withinDeadline` covers the whole `FileDataSource.resolve`, including the file listing, so it is not only a detection bound.
10. **`openCharacters` skips `SourceByteCountingContent`** in SequentialContentStack, so byte progress is missing on character reads (verified structurally; runtime effect unverified).
11. **Spike labels are left in production code**: "content streaming spike CS2" at `ConfiguredDataOpener.kt:69`, `ContentDataOpener.kt:9` and `DataOpenerLookup.kt:20`.
12. **The charset `auto` path is dead.** CharacterDecoder accepts it, but both capabilities reject it before it is reached.
13. **Detection opens a gzip file twice** (prefix read, close, reopen with gzip) and then the real read opens it again; at run time ParseWorker re-resolves every file (`resolveRead` with a fresh budget).
14. **The `ReaderProbeStrength.ContentSignature` doc ("magic bytes")** documents content identification as a supported public plugin contract.

### Not covered
- Not read in full: `ConfiguredDelimitedReader.kt` (787 lines; quote and escape handling and any further limits), `DelimitedRecordEncoder.kt`, `RecordWriterCapabilityRegistry` / `ConfiguredDelimitedWriterCapability`, `PlainTextReader`, `OwnedReaderDataCursor`, `FlatRecordBacked`.
- Common data model files not individually checked: `DataRef`, `DataPart`, `DataUnit`, `DataModelKeys`, `LegacyDataShapeBridge` (name suggests a compatibility shim; unverified), `NormalizedFormatHints`, `FormatHintMetadata`, `DetectionCandidateMetadata/Identity`, `InspectionCacheIdentity`, `CursorAdoptionIdentity`, `schema/*`.
- `AutomaticFormatResolverObserver`, `ReportDefinitionRepository` and the rest of `FileListingAction`.
- Tests: not reviewed beyond noting ConfiguredFormatExtensibilityTest ("third-party coding") and AutomaticFormatResolverMeasurementTest.
- Whether user notation under `notation/main` contains materialized formats with `contentCodings`, `bom: detect`, or LogicSourceWorker / legacy worker names: not checked.
- Provenance for LogicDataSource and the materialize intents: only keyword greps were done; a prompt using other wording may exist.

## Area B: Job runtime (kzen-auto)

### Summary
Area B is the Job execution engine and its Workers in `kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm`. That covers `server/exec/job/**` (JobRun, EngineJobControl, JobLogic and its compiler, the deadlock/stall monitor, channel topology, the E9 ownership ledger) and `server/objects/job/**` runtime (channels, every Worker, lending/borrowed elements, content/tar/Write, Format, recycling pools, work pool), plus the Job notation under `src/main/resources/notation/` (`auto-jvm/job/*.yaml`, `auto-common/common-job.yaml`, `auto-js/document/job-js.yaml`). It is about 20k lines, all of it written by agents after 2026-05. The baseline cce82db3f7 has no Job code. The user asked for the Job paradigm itself (2026-06-23 18:39). The worst problems:
1. Runs can break on real data. Run-time revalidation infers the output contract from a capped, time-limited sample (256 values / 30 s; FileSourceWorker samples only the first 256 files). ParseWorker then fails any later file that has a column outside that sample, and skips the superset pre-scan. This contradicts the user's "pre-checking the files, and already knows the full header set" (2026-10-07 17:45).
2. ParseWorker still identifies a selected file's format by sampling its bytes under a deadline, with concurrency, part-count and byte caps. Archive members are identified by name. So there are two different type rules, and one of them is the content sniffing the user asked to remove.
3. The engine has thresholds nobody asked for: a 50 ms polling deadlock monitor that fails the run after about 200 ms of "everyone blocked" and logs a stall after about 2 s; a 1 s timeout on external requests that the code itself calls a "KNOWN BOUNDED SEAM"; a 200 ms progress throttle; Preview capture limits (50 ms per item, 256 KiB, 2,000 nodes, 8 MiB window); and `migzThreads = 7`.
4. Spike leftovers and test seams are still in production code (`drainingEnabled`, `consumerCloseEnabled`, `TarGzEntryCursor.observer`/`produced`, `WriteWorker.encoderInterceptor`, `PreviewWorker.captureNanos`), and the legacy compatibility readers remain although the user said "we don't need backwards compatibility" (2026-09-24 16:37).
5. There are duplicate paths: CsvWriter, Export and Format+Write all write files; JobChannelTopology duplicates JobChannelDerivation; there are three Worker base classes, which the user called "artificial limitations" (corrected: the BE5 plan approved two minutes later, 2026-09-17T16:53 UTC, answers this: "Worker shapes are not restricted by the bases; `WorkerBase.drive` is the general contract", so the bases are approved as helpers; see row 15).

### Broken or false

1. **Runs fail on files beyond the validation sample, and revalidating cannot fix it.** `FileSourceWorker.kt:123` samples `files.take(design.budget.maxValues)` with maxValues 256 (`DesignReadBudget.kt:20-23`). The inferred shape is installed as `validatedShape` (`ParseWorker.kt:164`), which disables the superset pre-scan (`ParseWorker.kt:289`). `DataReadCore.fitShape` (`DataReadCore.kt:348-384`) then throws for any field outside the shape, telling the user to "validate the Job again". Revalidating samples the same first 256 files, so a run over more than 256 files where a later file has a new column cannot succeed. A budget that times out (30 s) has the same effect. This is verified by reading only; it was not executed.
2. **TarGzEntryCursor keeps every entry in production.** `TarGzEntryCursor.kt:51-52,93`: `internal val produced = ArrayList<TarEntryContent>()` and `produced.add(content)` on every `next()`, for test observation only. Memory grows with the number of archive members for the whole life of the cursor.
3. **Preview output is nondeterministic by design.** The 50 ms per-item wall-clock budget (`PreviewCapture.kt:154`, `PreviewWorker.kt:130-136`) truncates items on a loaded machine. The code comment admits it, and tests work around it by changing a static.
4. **Stale notation docs.** `job-worker.yaml:82-83` says the FileSourceWorker metadata includes "kind and Name-pattern values". `kind` was dropped (user 2026-09-25 16:14: "drop the kind field, it's always file") and Name pattern was deleted (2026-10-13 20:23). `FileValues.metadata` carries only name/path/size/modified/parent (`FileValues.kt`, `ExtractWorker.kt:92-101`). `job-jvm.yaml:4` "Concrete Workers live alongside in this folder once they exist (M1 step 4 onward)" is stale.
5. **The external query timeout is a self-declared workaround.** `JobRun.kt:377-394` documents it as a "KNOWN BOUNDED SEAM": a Worker slower than 1 s to answer makes the UI query return nothing.
6. **The deadlock monitor can fail a live run.** `JobDeadlockMonitor.kt:104-160` decides "deadlock" from a count over about 200 ms, not from a wait-for cycle, and it is turned off entirely while a duplex channel is open (`JobRun.kt:264`), so it is both over- and under-inclusive. This is verified from the code logic only; no false positive was reproduced.
7. **Extract ignores the file name.** `ExtractWorker.kt:105-122` opens every input as tar.gz, so a `.zip` or `.tar` input fails inside `GzipCompressorInputStream` with a generic I/O error instead of a by-name refusal.
8. **A deprecated API is still called** in the member-format path (`ParseWorker.kt:492-493`, `@Suppress("DEPRECATION")`).
9. **The EngineJobControl doc refers to the retired "old JobControlImpl"** (`EngineJobControl.kt` header).

### Not covered
- `exec/flow/**`, `exec/script/**` and `exec/report/**` (FlowRun, ScriptRunContext, ReportRun): these belong to the Script/Flow/Report areas.
- Not read line by line: FormulaWorker/FormulaSourceWorker, RunWorker body, SummaryWorker, PivotWorker body, ExploreWorker body, FilterWorker/ValueSetFilterWorker, PathProjectionWorker, WriterColumns, PathRowEvaluator, JobExpressionCompiler (499 lines), JobValidationDigest, SourceIngress/OpenedStream/AcquiredItem/RetriableCloseable/CallbackLeases, DuplexJobChannel, JobLaneDescriptor, and WritePathTemplate/Written. These were only grepped for numeric limits and test seams.
- Unverified: whether `JobResultCollector`'s ProducedBindingsBuilder is thread-safe, as its "Synchronized" comment claims; whether the kzen-sample plugins implement ResultYielder; and whether `DataReadCore.closeFallback` closing on the coroutine thread can block it.
- Design-time validation, contracts, DesignReader and the format detector internals belong to areas C and A; they are mentioned here only where the runtime depends on them.

## Area C: Job design-time, types and the data model

### Summary
This area is the Job's validation-before-Run pass and the type model under it. In kzen-auto that is `JobValidator`, `JobValidationCache` + `JobValidationDigest`, `server/data/design/**` (`DesignReader`, `DesignReadSession`, `DesignReadBudget`, `DesignEvidence`), `DesignShapeInference`, `JobLaneDescriptor`/`JobLaneSample`/`JobLaneAttempt`, the design-time `payloadFlow` of File/Parse/Read, `StepValidation`/`JobValidation` (the wire format), and the client's partial re-ask in `JobController`. In kzen-lib it is `exec/data/**`: about 7k main lines, plus about 6k test lines. None of it existed at the baselines (checked with `git ls-tree cce82db3f7` / `947c11368f`), so all of it is agent-written.

What the user asked for: design-time look-ahead at a schema (2026-08-20), typed payloads that flow between Workers (2026-07-21), a check before Run (2026-07-22), and a "minimally complex" design-time reading layer (2026-09-24 11:26). The unified data model started from a design doc the user brought in (2026-08-27 12:28).

Worst problems:
1. **A 2.5 s / 30 s / 256-value design-time budget nobody asked for.** The plan records these numbers as "answered as defaults" by the agent. The user later asked "why is there a 2.5s limit?" (2026-10-13 17:07). (corrected: a design-time bound itself, a cached design-read service and a small lane sample are in the problem definition the user approved, 2026-09-24T17:54 UTC, R4 "bounded in time and size ... never holds up editing"; only the numbers, the run budget, and the at-limit behaviour were left open as its Open question 2.)
2. **The "ask again until done" loop can run forever.** The editor re-asks every 1 s, and the uncommitted test harness uses `while(true)`. Neither terminates when the uncached reads at the start of a pass (File's folder listing, Read's manifest) alone use up the deadline.
3. **The 256-value cap combined with run-time agreement breaks large superset reads.** A run fails "fields outside the type validated before the run … validate the Job again" when a column first appears after file 256, and validating again cannot fix it.
4. **A false doc claim.** The plan says "a run whose revalidation was itself cut short has no validated type". In the code a partial type is passed to the run and enforced.
5. **Duplicate type representations and dead legacy members.** `JobLaneDescriptor` carries legacy members, `StepValidation` carries three encodings of the same type (one never read), there is a `DataShape` typealias shim, and the ScalarKind→class-name mapping exists twice.
6. **Several stacked caches (three layers just for part shapes), one of them an unbounded map.**
7. **kzen-lib generality nobody consumes.** Unions/variants, value algebra, the native type-resolver SPI and the adapter-registry SPI have no consumer outside kzen-lib. Snapshot display limits double as a hard cap on values leaving a Job run.

### Broken or false
1. **"Ask again until done" can loop forever.** `DesignReadSession.read` checks `remaining = deadline - elapsed` before every read and returns null (limited) once that is ≤ 0 (DesignReadSession.kt:103-107). Cached shapes are looked up only *inside* a read (DesignShapeInference.kt:73-75). File's folder listing (`design.observe` → `source.select`, FileSourceWorker.kt:109-112) and Read's manifest (ReadWorker.kt:452-455) are never cached across passes. So if the listing alone takes ≥ 2.5 s (a large or network folder), every pass is limited before Parse inspects anything, and no pass ever makes progress. The client re-asks every 1 s forever (JobController.kt:501-506), each time re-listing the folder. The uncommitted harness loops `while (true)` (ContentTestHarness.kt `validate`) with no exit. The same happens when one inspection is cancelled at a suspension point after the deadline, since its result is never stored. That second case is unverified: it depends on whether the opener suspends.
2. **The 256 cap breaks superset reads at run time.** Parse types from `files.take(256)` (FileSourceWorker.kt:122 → ParseWorker payloadFlow → DesignShapeInference). JobRun passes that contract as `outputContract` (JobRun.kt:312). `fitShape` rejects any later file with a field outside it (DataReadCore.kt:367-373: "...; validate the Job again"). Validating again reads the same first 256, so the run can never succeed while a new column first appears after file 256. A data-dependent false failure.
3. **False plan claim.** The plan's VM3 as-built says "A run whose revalidation was itself cut short has no validated type and reads as before." In the code, a partial inference with any candidates returns a typed contract (DesignShapeInference.kt:43-50). JobRun passes `contract` without checking `partial` (JobRun.kt:312). Only the cache refuses to reuse the entry (JobValidationCache.kt:301-302). So a run whose revalidation hit 30 s is held to the partial type.
4. **The plan's provenance text does not match the code.** R5 says "inferred from 41 of 256 files" (corrected: this label is in R4 of the problem definition the user approved, 2026-09-24T17:54 UTC, "A data-derived type is labelled as such, e.g. "inferred from 41 of 256 files"", so the code diverges from approved text, not just agent text). The code says "Inferred from N of M values" (DesignShapeInference.kt:46). Minor.
5. **`StepValidation.flatColumns` is dead on the wire**: written at JobValidator.kt:132, read nowhere in main.
6. **Doc in DesignReadSession** (lines 54-57) says "The value limit is a fixed bound, not a cut … in every pass alike". It is true that the value limit is not marked partial, but the provenance then reads like a complete inference, and #2 follows from it.
7. **`JobValidation` KDoc is stale** (JobValidation.kt:9-16, describes `flatColumns` and a null type for CSV lanes).
8. **Memory retention**: `DesignEvidence.recheck` closures capture the session's context and the Worker's source objects. Up to 100 cache entries keep old graph instances alive (JobValidationCache.kt:296-308). This is inferred from code; heap impact not measured.
9. **Unbounded memory map**: `SchemaCache.memory` is a plain `ConcurrentHashMap` with no eviction (SchemaCache.kt), while the `DesignReader` layer above it is size-bounded. The bounds are inconsistent.

### Not covered
- `JobChannelDerivation`, `JobChannelSynthesis`, `ChannelTypeDefiner`, `JobSignatureCapability`, `JobServeCapability`, `JobChannelPorts`, `JobConventions`: read only as far as JobValidator calls them. Their internal logic was not audited.
- `common/objects/document/job/path/**` (`PathBinding`, `ContractPathTree`, `WriterColumnSpec`, `ColumnPatterns`, `ProjectionPath`, `PathProjectionSpec`): not audited.
- `common/paradigm/**`: flow/* is mostly a rename of the baseline `dataflow/*` (user's original code, renamed by agents). job/api/* and logic/* (LogicCallGraph, RunTiming, MoveToRefusal) are new and were not audited.
- The `NotationMetadataReader` path and `WorkerDefinitionContext`: not audited.
- `SourceFormatResolutionBudget` / `SourceFormatResolutionPolicy` (overall timeout, cold-part semaphore) and `ReaderExecutionPolicies.inspection` (100 records, 30 s): seen, but owned by the data-reading area. They are more invented limits on the design-time path (ParseWorker.kt:261 `resolutionBudgetFactory.create().withinDeadline`).
- kzen-lib `DataBindings` / `BindingModel` / `ProducedBindingsBuilder`, `LiteralDataValues` (660 lines), `DataConstraint` (only `SymbolSet` is used by kzen-auto: ArchiveListingCursor.kt:49 and the JS display), `DataNode`/`DataState`/`ValueAccess`, the recursive-contract support: usage counted, internals not audited.
- Client-side type display (`DataContractPresentation`, `ContractTreeNode`, `SampledColumn`): only spot-checked.
- Whether a real inspection suspends (which decides the cancellation half of Broken #1): unverified.

## Area D — Job frontend (kzen-auto JS: `document/job/**`, `document/common/**`, `document/data/**`)

### Summary
Area: `kzen-auto/kzen-auto-js/src/jsMain/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/client/objects/document/{job,common,data}/**`, about 22,700 lines.
Almost all of it was written by agents. The only files that existed at the pre-agent baseline (kzen-auto `cce82db3f7`, 2026-05-01) are
`common/AttributePathValueEditor.kt`, `common/attribute/{AttributeEditor,AttributeEditorManager,AttributeEditorProps,DefaultAttributeEditor}.kt`,
`common/edit/{Boolean,MultiText,Select,Text}AttributeEditor.kt`, `common/edit/CommonEditUtils.kt`, `common/signature/LogicSignatureEditor.kt` and
`data/DataFormat{Controller,FieldAdd,FieldEdit}.kt`. All of `job/**` was written by agents.

Worst problems:
1. **The UI shows and builds on content-based format detection.** It shows "Detecting format…", "Automatic → X" and "based on file contents". It has a whole "correction" layer with an editor SPI: Make explicit, Lock columns, Inspect columns, per-file format and encoding overrides, and "Detect encoding". No prompt asked for any of this. It goes against principle 1.
2. **Hidden polling and retry guardrails.** The Job page re-fetches validation every 1 s for as long as any validation is "partial", which is caused by a server design-time read limit. Server validation retries a stale result every 100 ms, up to 10 times. No prompt asked for either.
3. **Channel tooling is broken or dead.**
   - The ribbon's Channel and Duplex Channel tools insert objects the page never shows.
   - `SelectChannelEditor` is bound on many ports but never mounted.
   - The client keeps a second copy of the server's channel-default precedence and of the literal defaults.
4. ~~**Requests from 2026-10-10 are only partly done.** The Write column editor still shows the "every payload column" summary and still renders the `meta` row. A plan says the `meta` row is "the user's call", but no prompt says that.~~ (corrected: the user reversed the `meta` request, "actually I changed my mind, I think the `meta` row is fine", 2026-10-10T18:56 UTC, and approved a plan that keeps the "pattern text" fallback when there is no upstream contract, 2026-10-10T19:00 UTC; see row 22. Only the string-prefix error routing remains.)
5. **Summary has two data paths.** The value pickers in the Filter and Pivot editors read only an in-memory store that is filled while a run is live. After the run ends or the page reloads, they have no values even though the summary is saved.
6. **Preview has invented caps and two render paths.** It requests a hardcoded limit of 200, truncates cells at 50 characters, and shows a "preview memory limit" message.

Rows: 33.

### Broken or false
- **Channel ribbon tools create invisible objects.** `JobController.kt:570-577` inserts under `channels`, but the render loop shows only Workers. The user cannot see or remove what they just inserted.
- **`SelectChannelEditor` never mounts.** It is bound in `job-worker.yaml`, but `WorkerDisplayDefault.kt:455` skips channel ports, so it is dead.
- **Filter and Pivot value pickers go empty after a run ends or the page reloads.** They read only `JobSummaryStore`, which is filled only by a live serve-channel pull during a run, while the persisted summary goes to the card only (`SummaryWorkerDisplay.kt`). Inferred from code; not reproduced at runtime.
- ~~**WriterColumnsEditor ignores two 2026-10-10T17:11 requests.**~~ Withdrawn (corrected).
  - "every payload column" (`:365,393`) shows only without an upstream contract, which the approved plan kept: "Without an upstream contract, it falls back to the pattern text as now." (2026-10-10T19:00 UTC).
  - The `meta` row (`:526-528,725`) stays by the user's own reversal: "actually I changed my mind, I think the `meta` row is fine -- I just didn't really understand how it worked" (2026-10-10T18:56 UTC), and the approved plan "Keep the `meta` row as it is." (19:00 UTC).

  The plan's "the user's call" claim is therefore true (a dialog answer, not a typed prompt).
- **The UI tells the user that formats are detected "based on file contents"** (`FileResolutionPresentation.kt:78`) and that encodings are detected ("Detect encoding", `DataFormatOptions.kt:63`). Both go against the user's stated rule (2026-10-12, 2026-10-14).
- **Validation that stays `partial` is polled every second, forever** (`JobController.kt:165,499-507`).
- **`JobProgressStore.kt:25-26` reads a path that its own comment says is "unwritten".** This is dead compat code.
- **File read progress is guessed from selection order** (`FileReadProgress.kt:257-296`). It would be wrong if the reader ever reads out of order, in parallel, or skips files. It also matches files by path string.
- **Preview's "Larger sample" is enabled only during a run**, and the text "within the preview memory limit" (`PreviewSampleView.kt:214`) exposes an internal server cap.
- **Error routing in WriterColumnsEditor depends on English message prefixes** (`startsWith("Allow")` / `"Exclude"`, `:801,816`). Rewording a server message would misroute the error.
- **`docs/js-architecture.md` claims I checked were accurate.** `description:` is drawn under the field (`AttributeEditorManager.kt:48,127-179`), and `DebouncedSubmitter` uses a sequence counter instead of lodash `.pending()`. No false claim was found in the parts I checked.

### Not covered
Read only partially, or not at all:
- **`job/edit`:** KotlinFieldEditor, SelectPartRoleEditor, SelectDataSourceEditor, AddNameForm, ColumnDraftField, ContractPathPicker, PathProjectionEditor/EntryRow, JobUpstreamSchema, FormulaMapEditor/Row, SortSpecEditor, ExportSpecEditor. For PivotSpecEditor and ValueSetFilterEditor I covered only their data source.
- **`job`:** JobObjectSlot, JobCardRowRegistry, WorkerDisplayManager, PreviewWorkerDisplay, output/FormatWorkerDisplay.
- **`common`:**
  - FileBrowser, FileBrowserToggle, DirectoryEditor, KotlinCodeArea (902 lines).
  - signature/*: LogicSignatureEditor, ContextSignatureEditor, ResultSignatureEditor, LogicTypePicker.
  - select/*, YamlEditor, raw/*, scope/*, dragdrop/*, valid/ExpressionValidationIndicator.
  - The rest of attribute/*.
- **`data/schema/*`:** only skimmed.
- **`docs/js-architecture.md`:** I checked only the claims above, not all 594 lines.
- **Runtime behaviour:** nothing was run, so every behavioural statement is from reading code.

## Area E: the Report document in kzen-auto (audit since baseline `cce82db3f7`, 2026-05-01)

**Summary.** Area E is the Report document in three places: the JVM side (`kzen-auto-jvm/.../server/objects/report/**`, plus the Report pieces moved to `server/data/**` and `server/exec/report/**`), common specs (`kzen-auto-common/.../objects/document/report/**`) and the JS editor (`kzen-auto-js/.../objects/document/report/**`). It adds up to roughly 150 Kotlin files. Most of the original Report engine (FlatFileRecord pipeline, LMAX disruptor, PivotBuilder, IndexedCsvTable, ValueSummaryBuilder, FileFlatDataStream) is still the user's own code. The worst problems:
- **(a) Job still reaches into Report everywhere.** The user said Job should not poke into Report (2026-08-21) and that Report will become obsolete. Yet the Job workers and editors import about 15 Report classes and about 10 Report specs. Several of those specs were changed to carry Job-only concepts (SortSpec, standalone export paths, `pivotPath`). The "DS0" package move only renamed packages.
- **(b) Report's read and write paths are duplicated by Job.** Report decides gzip by name and reads it with the JDK. Job reads gzip through a separate Commons Compress wrapper. Gzip writing (MiGz with 7 threads) is coded twice. CSV writing is coded twice too (commons-csv `CsvExportFormatter` and a hand-written `DelimitedRecordEncoder` that "follows it byte for byte").
- **(c) Report was ported onto the new execution engine through adapters.** These include a trace-handle shim with no-op methods, a reserved `$trace-path` address, a JS run-merge workaround and a MULTI-producer change made to quiet a test assertion. Hosted Report is admittedly broken for preview and run directories (master plan row 45 is still open).
- **(d) An invented 200 ms trace throttle with no final flush.**
- **(e) The simple `columns.csv` column cache was replaced.** The new design is a schema cache going through a "Legacy" bridge plus a code-location fingerprint. Its key does not cover what its own doc says it covers.
- **(f) `kzen-auto/docs/architecture.md` makes several false claims about Report.**

| # | Mechanism | Where | What it actually does (verified) | Provenance (+quote, timestamp) | Principle issues | Verdict | Blast radius |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Report gzip and format by file name | `kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/server/objects/report/exec/input/connect/file/FileFlatDataStream.kt:17,24,42`; `kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/server/data/ReportDefinitionRepository.kt` (`find` uses `dataLocation.innerExtension()`, `.gz`-aware, `kzen-auto-common/.../DataLocation.kt:85`) | Decides gzip by `location.name.endsWith(".gz")`, then reads with JDK `GZIPInputStream` (128 KiB buffer) wrapped in `BOMInputStream`. The format comes from the inner extension. No content sniffing. This is baseline code; agents changed only the import/package. | User's original (pre-2026-05) | None. This is the model the user described on 2026-10-12: "the .gz tells us that it's a GZip file". | Keep. This is the rule Job should share. | Every Report input. |
| 2 | Second gzip read path (Job) | `kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/server/data/content/coding/GzipSequentialByteContent.kt` vs #1 | Job reads gzip through a Commons Compress wrapper, separately from Report's JDK reader. ### Broken or false

1. **No final trace publish.** `ReportOutputTrace.kt:21-27,42-47` throttles to 200 ms and has no flush on completion. The live counter can show a stale count until the UI switches to `rowCount` after the run (`kzen-auto-js/.../report/output/OutputTableController.kt:178-181`).
2. **Hosted Report is broken for the UI path.** `ReportLogic.kt:28-33` KDoc: the preview handler is on its own node while the client addresses the root, and the run directory is stamped with the host's run id. Master plan row 45 is still open (☐). The user said on 2026-08-03 11:54 that hostability is "a key design point".
3. **Stale KDoc references.** `ReportRun.kt` refers to `tech.kzen.auto.server.objects.report.ReportExecution` (renamed or deleted). `ReportLogic.kt:19` refers to `ReportDocument.define`, which does not exist.
4. **`kzen-auto/docs/architecture.md` (agent-authored after baseline) is false:**
   - Line 675 lists `filterIndex | FilterIndex | Persistent column-value index backing report filtering`, but FilterIndex was deleted.
   - Line 737 names a "filter index" storage area, which is now the SchemaCache area.
   - Line 18 says "Plugin SPI: how third-party plugins extend reports", which contradicts line 676 (plugins no longer extend Reports).
   - Lines 714-722 say "never Thread.sleep or do blocking I/O inside a ReportPipelineStage", yet CompressedExportWriter, a ReportPipelineStage, does blocking file I/O.
5. **`ColumnValue` equals/hashCode contract is broken.** Equality uses an epsilon while the hash uses the text, so values that are equal can hash differently. This matters in hash-based grouping. The bug predates the audit window but is now documented only in a comment, not fixed (`ColumnValue.kt`).
6. **The schema-cache key does not cover reader code.** The Report key uses `CodeFingerprint.host` instead of `forReader(...)`, unlike what the SchemaCacheKey doc describes (`SchemaCacheKey.kt:33-49`). A reader change inside a plugin or class directory that the host fingerprint does not see can serve stale columns. Unverified in practice.
7. **Conflicting DataType→metadata mappings.** `CalculatedColumnEval.toTypeMetadata()` maps an Integer wider than 64 bits to Long, while `LegacyDataShapeBridge.toLegacyTypeMetadata` maps it to BigInteger. Formula type inference can disagree with schema display.
8. **Divergent number parsing in summaries.** `ValueSummaryBuilder.add(String)` vs `add(FlatFileRecordField)` handle NaN, infinity and edge cases differently. The exact divergent inputs are unverified.
9. **The DS0 "move" claims to separate Job from Report** (master plan row 49), but `server/data/*` still imports `objects.report.*`, so the claim is false in substance.
10. **`ReportDefinitionRepository.classLoaderHandle` ignores its `coordinates` argument**, while callers still compute them. This is dead data flow.

### Not covered

- kzen-lib `NotationCodec` / `CodecAttributeDefiner` internals (area H).
- The Job workers' own logic beyond their Report imports (area B); `GzipSequentialByteContent` details (area A).
- ManagedStorageRegistry / storage-manager budgets and the code-cache (area G).
- The shared JS `FileBrowser` component internals (area D).
- Deep correctness of PivotBuilder / IndexedCsvTable / ValueSummaryBuilder beyond the diffed lines.
- `kzen-auto-plugin` SPI as used by Report decoders.
- Report test files (`kzen-auto-jvm/src/test/**/report/**`): not reviewed. In particular, which test triggers the LMAX SINGLE-producer assertion (#15) is unverified.
- Whether the "direct user request (2026-08-17)" for the download refactor (#25) is in prompts.md was not checked.

## Area F — Script, Logic, Flow, Target, Custom (+ plugin doc, context, bridge JS)

### Summary
Area F covers the kzen-auto Script/Logic/Flow/Target/Custom document families: the JVM objects (`server/objects/{script,logic,flow,target,custom,plugin}`, about 5k lines), their common models (`common/objects/document/{script,logic,flow,target,custom,plugin}`), the JS editors (about 25k lines, most of them new since baseline `cce82db3f7`), and the Kotlin expression compile path (`service/compile/*`, `ScriptValidationCache`, `FormulaStep` inference). At baseline the area was `sequence/` and `graph/`. Almost everything in it was written since then by agents, about 33k changed lines across 235 files.

Most top-level features trace to the user: rename rewriting of Kotlin expressions, the syntax-highlighted expression editor, the static type check, ForEach partial values, continue/break, Context/Resource, Custom tasks, and the browser delays. The problems sit inside those features:
- **A real rename bug.** Renaming a step or Job parameter rewrites *any* scalar text attribute that happens to contain the name as a word, not only code attributes.
- **Invented limits nobody asked for.** ForEach keeps only 100 journal entries of 120 chars each. Trace snapshots are capped at depth 16, 256 elements and 100 ms. Trace display is capped at 2048 chars. The compiled-code disk cache has a hard-coded 1 GiB budget with LRU eviction. The validation cache holds 100 entries.
- **Hidden behaviour that changes values.** ForEach returns an empty list whenever static analysis says nothing reads the loop. FlowRun treats a 50 ms wall-clock gap as "the user is stepping", and a mutable test seam lives in main code.
- **Leftovers.** There is a compatibility warning for the retired `context.slots` key, and a raw string-keyed resource API that only tests call.
- **Unrequested pieces.** Target match policies `first` / `nth` / `best` have no UI and appear only in a test YAML. `RunLogicVertex2` is a duplicate archetype added to avoid a grid-geometry change.

### Broken or false
1. **Rename rewrites non-code text (bug, verified by reading the code; no test covers it).** `KzenAutoCodeReferenceRewriter.kt:66-74` takes every `ValueAttributeDefinition` scalar attribute as a candidate. `KotlinExpressionAnalyzer.referencedIdentifiers` treats plain prose as identifiers. So a Script with a step `query` and a later in-scope `BrowserWriteStep` whose `text: query` (`BrowserWriteStep.kt:15`, `private val text: String`) gets its typed text rewritten when the step is renamed. The same happens to `BrowserGetStep.location` (`BrowserGetStep.kt:17`). In a Job, renaming a parameter rewrites every lexically matching scalar attribute of every Worker document-wide (`:91-94`), for example a column-name or path attribute equal to the parameter name. The class KDoc (`:18-20`) claims it rewrites "code attributes (Formula / DoWhile / Filter `where` / FormulaSource `code`)", which is false: the code has no such filter. Tests (`kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm/src/test/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/common/objects/document/script/model/KzenAutoCodeReferenceRewriterTest.kt:25-93`) only cover code attributes.
2. **ForEach can silently return `[]`** (`ForEachStep.kt:111,203,227`) whenever `isValueReferenced` misses a consumer. The value then depends on static analysis, not on what the loop computed. It is not verified whether a Script whose result is the loop (read by a calling RunStep) counts as "referenced".
3. **Trace content depends on load.** `FlowRun.snapshotMillis` (100 ms wall-clock, `FlowRun.kt:96-105,747`) makes a slow reader trace values as "unavailable". The KDoc itself admits tests must lift it. The 50 ms "stepping" proxy (`FlowRun.kt:91,186`) misclassifies slow free-running vertices as stepping.
4. **Validation-cache contract hole (agent-recorded, unverified by test).** The closure key (`LogicValidationDigest.kt:26-32`) does not cover documents a third-party step reads on its own, so validation can be stale.
5. **False/stale comment:** `LogicContextConventions` / `ContextSignatureEditor` keep machinery for a key that no real notation uses (only the test fixture).

### Not covered
- `exec/script/*` and `exec/flow/*` engine internals beyond the snapshot/throttle constants (ScriptReplayState, jump analysis, replay barriers). Only spot-read.
- `LogicContextAnalysis` (full), `ScriptJumpAnalysis`, the IfStep/DoWhile validators in depth.
- The large JS controllers, not read in full: `ContextsController` (≈896 lines), `ScriptExecutionMargin` (≈902), `ScriptProgressStore` (≈555), the bridge JS, the custom/target JS stores.
- kzen-lib's binding/definition engine that these objects rely on (Area H).
- Plugin runtime and loading (Area G), storage-management UI and the evictor (`StorageLruEvictor`) beyond the code-cache budget wiring.
- Whether `TargetLocateAction` / `ScreenshotTaker` contain further limits: only skimmed (all matches, no limit found).

## Area G — kzen-auto infrastructure (scrub audit)

### Summary
Area G is kzen-auto's plumbing:
- the JVM context and runtime: `KzenAutoContext`, `KzenAutoRuntime`, plugin scopes, the compatibility kit, work-root claims and `KzenAutoHost`;
- the Ktor entry point and REST/SSE routes, the compile, exec, storage, target, vision and webdriver services, util and paradigm;
- kzen-auto-common `util`/`api`/`service`;
- the JS client services, ribbon, sidebar and wrappers;
- the `kzen-auto-plugin` SPI module, the `kzen-auto-test` e2e harness, and the build files.

That is roughly 9.2k JVM lines, 2.8k common lines, 9.3k JS lines, 1k plugin lines and 0.9k harness lines. Almost all of it was written or rewritten by agents after the cce82db3f7 baseline (224 files, +14k/−3.6k lines in this slice alone).

Worst problems:
1. **The plugin SPI ships a content-sniffing probe API** (`ReaderProbe*`, `ReaderProbeStrength.ContentSignature` = "magic bytes"). It directly contradicts principle 1 and is live in production (#30).
2. **kzen-sample `spring-embed` no longer compiles against current kzen-auto.** `KzenAutoConfig` gained an 11th constructor parameter (`codeCacheRoot`, 2026-09-25) that exists only for tests, and the Java host still calls the 10-argument form (#9).
3. **Invented limits and timeouts throughout:**
   - a 1 GiB compiled-code budget with an LRU evictor that walks the whole cache after every compile (#5);
   - a 32-entry instance cache with a second unbounded mode and an opt-out attribute nobody uses (#46);
   - a 10 s close timeout (#55);
   - SSE probe/watchdog/heartbeat numbers and a push-off latch that lasts the life of the page (#18);
   - a 30 s cap in slow motion that likely breaks steps longer than 30 s (#62);
   - fixed screen scales in image matching (#57);
   - trace display caps (#68).
4. **Test-only code in production:**
   - `KzenAutoContext.forTest`, `TestContextLeakGuard`, the shared test code cache and `codeCacheRoot`;
   - spike toggles (`drainingEnabled`, `consumerCloseEnabled`) read on Job hot paths;
   - `PluginCompatibilityKit` (410 lines);
   - test-only `WorkUtils` factories.
5. **Speculative extension points with no real second user:**
   - `TargetTypeLocator.register`, used only by a test;
   - `WebDriverSupport.resourceKey`, zero references;
   - `PluginSpiVersion`, never bumped, which contradicts "there is no backwards compatibility";
   - `FormatAuthoringCapability`;
   - a one-implementation `ReportDefinitionRepository`.

Timestamps below are taken from `prompts.md`, which records local time (UTC−4). `user-requests.md` records the same messages in UTC (`T` form, 4 h later); where a quote was located there, it has been converted to local time.

### Broken or false
- **spring-embed no longer compiles against current kzen-auto.**
  - `KzenAutoConfig` (kzen-auto/kzen-auto-jvm/src/main/kotlin/tech/kzen/auto/server/context/KzenAutoConfig.kt:8-45) is a Kotlin data class with 11 parameters, without `@JvmOverloads`, so Java must pass all 11.
  - kzen-sample/spring-embed/backend/src/main/java/tech/kzen/sample/embed/workspace/KzenWorkspace.java:88-98 passes 10 (… `workRoot, services, false`), so `services` lands in the `codeCacheRoot: Path?` slot.
  - `codeCacheRoot` was added in 365e5450 (2026-09-25); KzenWorkspace.java last changed 6c4ee48 (2026-09-09).
  - Verified by signature; no build was run (read-only). The stale copy at C:\Users\ostro\IdeaProjects\kzen-sample-embed-spring has the same call.
- **The code-cache "can never be stale" comment is false** (CachedKotlinCompiler.kt:81-82). The key ignores the compiler/kzen version and the plugin classpath (ScriptKotlinCompiler.kt:177), and entries persist across restarts and across test contexts.
- **Slow motion likely misbehaves on steps longer than 30 s** (ClientLogicGlobal.kt:831-849; #62).
- **The SSE probe latch** (ClientLogicGlobal.kt:170, 244): one late first message disables push until the page reloads.
- **`PluginSpiVersion` is a fake gate.** It is still 1 after SPI changes (c09cb45b, 62c3782b, 11208c5b), and a manifest without `spi` is accepted.
- **False or stale docs and comments:**
  - The `PluginCoordinate` KDoc (kzen-auto-plugin/.../model/PluginCoordinate.kt:8-10) says the plugin module has zero dependencies on kzen-auto-common and kzen-lib; kzen-auto-plugin/build.gradle.kts:20-21 has `api` dependencies on both.
  - `TargetTypeLocator.kt:8-10` says third-party types call `register`; only a test does.
  - `WebDriverSupport.kt:15-18` describes a shared key that nothing uses.
  - `PathPatternSubstitution.kt:4-5` says it is "shared by Report exports and Job writers"; only Report uses it.
  - The `StorageManagerController` KDoc lists an "indexes" area that no longer exists.
  - `DocumentBridge.kt:23` says the Globals were replaced, yet `ViewModeGlobal` and `InsertionGlobal` still exist.
  - kzen-auto-js/build.gradle.kts:144 says "CommonJS output"; the entry is `.mjs` (ES modules).
  - kzen-auto-common/build.gradle.kts:43-44 says no serializers are written in this module; 39 files are `@Serializable`.
- **Umbrella kzen/AGENTS.md is false about `useCommonJs()`.** It says `useCommonJs()` "must stay", but kzen-auto-js/build.gradle.kts:28 and kzen-launcher-js use `useEsModules()` (since d7239686, 2026-07-07).
- **kzen-auto/AGENTS.md says the plugin root defaults to `plugins/` "beside the module".** The code (KzenAutoMain.kt:130-131) resolves `plugins/` *under* `moduleRoot`, or under the cwd.
- **kzen-auto/AGENTS.md documents content detection as intended behaviour.** Its gotcha says "a binary format is detected by content ... an exact signature (header row, magic) returns `ReaderProbeStrength.ContentSignature`". That contradicts principle 1; it is the doc side of #30.
- **The `ContentNegotiation` install** (KzenAutoMain.kt:165-169) is described as for "a future call.respond(dto)", and nothing uses it.
- **The download header is knowingly malformed** for non-ASCII names (KzenAutoMain.kt:548-550); safety depends on caller convention.
- **The `ReaderProbeObserver` contract is not honoured.** It requires counts no greater than the record limit; kzen-sample `WorldCitiesReaderCapability` always reports 1.
- **The `ServiceEnvironmentValidation` kdoc mentions re-validation "after plugin load"**; nothing re-runs it.
- **Tests write into the shared `../work` folder** (JobExpressionCompilerTest.kt:48; CalculatedColumnEvalTest.kt:431-503), despite the per-context work-root design.

### Not covered
- The body of `ServerLogicController.kt` (1061 lines) beyond constants and timeouts; `LogicHandler` internals (Logic/Script area).
- `TargetLocator.kt` lines 80-529 (preview exclusion, match policies) and the `RgbGrid` changes.
- The Notation*Commands handlers beyond wiring. Four have no JS caller, but they predate the baseline (the user's own).
- JS bodies of HeaderController, RibbonController, HeaderRunController, SidebarFolder/Document, DocumentNameEditor, SidebarCreateSubMenu, CropperWrapper, MuiAutocompleteField, ClientStateGlobal, NavigationGlobal (only skimmed).
- `common/util/data/FilePath.kt`, `DataLocation`, `DataLocationInfo` (data area); provenance of each `CommonRestApi` endpoint.
- Whether each npm pin is still needed under KGP 2.4.0 (needs a lock refresh, which is not allowed read-only); the Java edits in kzen-auto-plugin buffers (diffstat only).
- The kzen-auto-test notation YAML in depth.
- Stale directories outside the composite (C:\Users\ostro\IdeaProjects\kzen-sample-embed-spring, kzen-sample-plugin), which look like pre-consolidation copies. Not audited; the user's own directories.
- Two pre-baseline dead files left as user code: `service/plugin/PluginRepository.kt` (empty) and `ProcessorDefinitionSignature.kt` (commented out).

## Area H — kzen-lib (common / jvm / js / reflect-ksp)

### Summary

kzen-lib is the multiplatform core. It holds notation, graph, the YAML dialect, reflection and KSP, and, new since baseline `947c11368f`, the execution engine (RunEngine, Execution, CountingDispatcher, context and disposal) and the exec data model (DataType / DataShape / DataContract / DataSnapshot, native object access, adapters, metadata overlays).
- **Size.** 143 commits since baseline. Main sources +19,388/−4,075 lines, tests +16,109/−783, docs +1,586. Main `.kt` is now about 30.5k lines. The largest files are RunEngine.kt (1,891), DefaultNativeTypeResolver (1,030), YamlParser (1,117), LiteralDataValues (660), NotationReducerRefactor (645), DataContract (626) and logic-spec.md (1,178).
- **Worst problem 1: invented snapshot caps.** The default `SnapshotPolicy` (depth 64, 100k elements, 1 MB text, 10 MB binary, 5 s) is used at the Job result boundary, so a large result fails the Job. That directly contradicts the 2026-10-09 12:26 instruction. A wall-clock `ReadBudget` and a thread-local `ReadPreparation` tally were added on top of it.
- **Worst problem 2: broken cycle detection.** Snapshot cycle detection rejects any container that is shared (seen twice), not only real cycles, and it is O(n²).
- **Worst problem 3: wrong trace data.** Trace `time` is stamped when the trace is queried, not when the event happened; the user asked for event time on 2026-06-17. Trace `objectStableId` is the document id, not the step id.
- **Worst problem 4: code with no product consumer.** Examples:
  - an empty `closeDataAdapters()`
  - `retainTrace` / `observeFrames` / `observeResets`
  - the raw-string resource layer (contradicts 2026-07-31)
  - deprecated shims
  - a `retained`-bindings API nothing in the product calls or closes
  - a DataAdapter registry, a NativeTypeDescriber SPI and a sensitive-redaction mode, none with a production implementation
- **Worst problem 5: the YAML save path hides failures.** The format-preserving save swallows every exception and silently rewrites the whole file. Two docs claims about the YAML dialect are false.

Verification: B-1/2/9, A-1/A-31, C-2/C-4/C-24 and the architecture.md:132 claim were re-checked by hand. All other rows were checked in code by sub-auditors, with file:line references. The audit was read-only: no builds or tests were run.

Row prefixes: **A** = engine / logic / trace / context, **B** = exec data model, **C** = notation / graph / YAML / reflection / KSP / build. 99 rows.
